People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. My appetite is just off and I'm skipping meals.\n\nDoctor: Has your clothing become noticeably looser over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. A few pairs of pants are a bit baggy now. But... like I was saying, that's really what finally made me come in to see you. My appetite being all over the place for months, and losing this weight without trying. Just... skipping dinner or picking at crackers because cooking for myself feels pointless. I go through the motions, I guess. Like... watering the garden, or showing up to my book club. But... I don't know, it just feels kind of flat lately. Sometimes I end up leaving early, or... I just don't start at all. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting any pleasure out of the things you're still doing?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. Like I just said, I'm mostly just... going through the motions. I don't really get anything out of it right now. It just stays flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if guilt is the right word. I just... I think about my marriage a lot. I keep replaying it in my head, over and over. I just keep telling myself I must have... failed at something basic, you know? Like I did something wrong. Even though... I mean, part of me knows relationships are more complicated than that. But... yeah. I just think about it a lot.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost over these months?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know the exact number. I haven't really been weighing myself. But... I have lost some without trying to. \n\nMy appetite has just been all over the place for months. That's actually... Cooking just for myself... it just feels kind of pointless right now. So... yeah, I've lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight loss is more than 5% of your original body weight?\n\nPatient: I... I really couldn't say if it's five percent. Like I just said, I haven't stepped on a scale. I just know I'm losing it without trying because... well, because of what I just mentioned. My appetite is just off and I'm skipping meals.\n\nDoctor: Has your clothing become noticeably looser over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. A few pairs of pants are a bit baggy now. But... like I was saying, that's really what finally made me come in to see you. My appetite being all over the place for months, and losing this weight without trying. Just... skipping dinner or picking at crackers because cooking for myself feels pointless. So... yes, I guess my clothes are a little looser.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost weight to the point where others have noticed?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if anyone else has noticed. I don't really ask. But... like I've been saying, the whole reason I'm here is because my appetite has been so unpredictable for months. Skipping dinner, losing weight without meaning to... or just eating cereal because cooking for one feels so pointless. That's... that's what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight loss been significant enough that you've had to buy smaller clothes?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't gone shopping for new clothes. \n\nI feel like I keep saying the same thing, but... that's really what finally made me come in to see you. Just my appetite being all over the place for months now. There are stretches where I just skip dinner entirely, which is why I'm losing weight without trying to. And other nights I just pick at cereal or crackers... mostly because cooking for myself just feels pointless. But no, I haven't bought anything smaller.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight loss is affecting your overall health?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't thought about it. \n\nI... I know I keep repeating myself, but I don't know what else to say. That's really what finally made me come in to see you. My appetite has just been all over the place for months now. I have these stretches where I just skip dinner entirely, which is why I'm losing the weight without trying. And other times I just pick at some crackers or a bowl of cereal, just because cooking for myself feels pointless. I don't know if it's affecting my health, it's just... what's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight loss is affecting your energy levels?\n\nPatient: I guess so. I am pretty tired lately. Even on days when my caseload at the hospital isn't unusually heavy... I just feel like I'm dragging my body around all day. And by the time I get home, I'm too exhausted to cook, or stretch, or even answer personal messages. I don't know if that's because of the weight loss, but... yeah, my energy is low.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you appear underweight or overly thin to yourself when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Not really. I haven't really noticed looking overly thin.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
